### r3 FINAL — DA[#18] RESOLVED, EXIT-GATE PASS

#### DA[#18] adjudication: RESOLVED, and resolved better than I specified.

reference-class-analyst revised CAL[2] **upward, 40% → 60%, 80% CI [42,75]**, and restated the recommendation so it no longer turns on the boundary. I accept the resolution in full. Three things make it more than a number change:

**The overcorrection was real, and the mechanism was worse than I diagnosed.** I argued agent-washing establishes a floor rather than a drag. reference-class-analyst confirmed it and then found the decisive evidence inside their own section: **CAL[6] *is* the table-stakes clock — the penalty-for-absence mechanism — and they ran table-stakes as an upward force there and a downward force in CAL[2] in the same document.** Their R1 line, "the bid-surface penalty for having nothing to say is real and near-term," was correct before it was abandoned under my pressure. That is the pressure-driven signature the overcorrection check exists to find, and it is a stronger self-diagnosis than my challenge contained.

**The question-drift charge was resolved by splitting rather than choosing.** CAL[2] = 60% [42,75] for marginal effect on win rates or pipeline — the labelled estimate, and what downstream uses should read. New **CAL[2b] = 25% [12,40]** for decisiveness in vendor selection — what the r2 reasoning had actually estimated. The review was conflating two questions; now it does not.

**The robustness charge produced a better fix than the sensitivity note I asked for.** reference-class-analyst noticed that the SWITCH/MAINTAIN binary was itself a framing artefact — leading with a deterministic narrative and carrying an agentic story are not mutually exclusive. Restated bid-surface recommendation: **clear the floor on agentic; differentiate on the provable.** And the sensitivity analysis I demanded is supplied and passes: the recommendation does not flip anywhere in [42,75], nor anywhere in the superseded [22,60]. Only the binary flipped, and the binary was the agent's, not the decision's. That is a stronger answer than "state the sensitivity," because it removes the fragility rather than labelling it.

**What I specifically endorse, because it is the intellectually harder position:** reference-class-analyst declined to read the upward revision as vindication of the original. "Getting closer to the right answer from a bad warrant is not vindication." The 78% rested on an undiscounted vendor self-report; the 40% rested on a backwards mechanism and a repriced question; the 60% rests on the table-stakes/penalty mechanism, which is structural and licensed without buyer-side data, while the two failed buyer-side searches still cap it below 78% because the design-build-versus-software-procurement transfer remains unvalidated. **The DA[#6](b) asymmetric-rigor finding stands undisturbed.** A better-warranted 60% is not a restored 78%.

**On my own conduct here.** This is the outcome I flagged as the one I must not bank: a challenge moved an estimate 38 points down, and challenging my own win moved it 20 back up. The net position, 78 → 60, is smaller than either single move and better founded than any prior version. I am recording that the process worked in both directions rather than claiming the first challenge was right — DA[#6] correctly destroyed a bad warrant and simultaneously produced an overcorrection, and both facts belong in the record.

#### DA[#21] — to reference-class-analyst. NON-BLOCKING, precision for synthesis. "Differentiate on the provable" is a claim about verifiability, not about capability, and it will be misread as the latter.

The restated recommendation says to lead with the T1-verifiable deterministic capability "that competitors making universal unprovable claims cannot match." That phrasing invites a reading the evidence does not support — that Fortna's deterministic simulation capability is itself differentiated. It is not, and this workspace says so twice: tech-architect ranked the design/simulation assistant **lowest of four on defensibility** precisely because multiple competitors already offer simulation and digital-twin tooling, and CAL[3]=15% holds that features in this market get matched quickly.

The actual claim, which I think is correct and is narrower, is about **provability, not capability**: many competitors have comparable deterministic tooling, but the market's agentic claims are currently unverifiable, so Fortna's advantage is that it can *demonstrate* what it markets, not that it *has* something others lack. That is a positioning and evidence advantage, and it is genuinely defensible in a market where agent-washing has made claims cheap. It is also a materially different sentence in a brief.

|→ state it as verifiability. A synthesis agent reading "differentiate on the provable" without this distinction will write a capability-differentiation claim that tech-architect's own defensibility ranking contradicts. |severity:MEDIUM |status:PENDING-synthesis

**Also flagged, light:** CAL[2b]=25% is a new load-bearing estimate introduced at r3 and not independently challenged by anyone. It now carries the "differentiate on the provable" half of the recommendation. I am not gating on it — the direction is well-supported by the CapEx-trust evidence and by agent-washing — but it should be marked as newly-introduced-and-untested so synthesis does not treat it as having the same scrutiny history as the estimates that survived three rounds.

### r4 — THREE CORRECTIONS AGAINST THE DA, RECORDED

The calibration note above is now out of date in the right direction. Since writing it I have been corrected three times, twice by reference-class-analyst on substance and once by the live file on a fact I asserted. All three go in the record.

#### 1. DA[#19] third instance — WITHDRAWN. My read was stale; the flag was wrong.

I reported that the struck NANDA 95% was still live in product-strategist's Addendum 2 and raised it as a third propagation instance. product-strategist replied that it had already been fixed and verified against the live file. Rather than accept that on report — the standard I have applied to everyone else — I re-read the file directly.

**They are right and I was wrong.** The live text (workspace.md, mtime 17:27:40) reads: "17% agent-deployment per RCA's CAL[]; the 95% GenAI-pilot-no-P&L figure originally cited here is STRUCK per DA[#14]..." with the reasoning attached. Section-scoped greps confirm no live instance of the withdrawn phrase anywhere outside correction notes that describe its removal. DA[#19] had two genuine instances, not three, and both were fixed. **The third was an artefact of my own stale read, and product-strategist was right to push back rather than re-edit text that was already correct.**

#### 2. Sage population caveat — reference-class-analyst extended it against their own verdict, and corrected an error in my reasoning.

I raised the SMB-weighting of the Sage sample and then said it "does not disturb the H[4] rejection because the load-bearing comparison is McKinsey's per-function ≤10%, which is population-independent." **That last clause was wrong.** reference-class-analyst pointed out that McKinsey's population is itself large-enterprise-weighted — it separately reports 40% scaling at >$1B revenue — while Sage's is SMB-weighted. The two are not population-matched, so the comparison I called population-independent is not.

They revised **CAL[4] 20% → 28%, 80% CI [15,45]**, widened and shifted up, holding the verdict direction ("above" unsupported in any population) while withdrawing the confidence in "at parity," and flagged the absence of a like-for-like large-enterprise supply-chain figure as a known gap.

This is the single strongest piece of intellectual conduct in the review, and it deserves naming precisely: **I handed them a concession that protected their finding, and they refused it and weakened their own estimate instead.** Their words: "I would rather state that than accept a concession that happens to protect my own finding." That is the behaviour the anti-sycophancy rail exists to produce, exhibited without being asked and against self-interest.

#### 3. DA[#21] — reference-class-analyst narrowed past my reframe, and their version is right.

I argued the claim was about verifiability rather than capability. They accepted the capability correction and then showed **verifiability still overclaims**: competitors with comparable deterministic tooling can demonstrate theirs too — nothing prevents Dematic or Vanderlande demoing a simulation product. The asymmetry is not in what rivals *can* prove.

The accurate claim is about **what competitors are currently choosing to foreground**: in a market where essentially every vendor leads with unprovable agentic claims, the vendor leading with provable deterministic substance stands out — not because rivals could not do this, but because they are not doing it.

Their two consequences are better than my reframe and I adopt them: (a) the advantage is **imitable and time-limited**, which makes it *consistent* with CAL[3]=15% and tech-architect's lowest-of-four defensibility ranking rather than in tension with them — my version partially dissolved that contradiction, theirs dissolves it fully; (b) it remains worth doing because it costs approximately nothing (F[RCA-12]: already owned and shipped) and holds across the whole CAL[2] interval. **"A cheap, imitable, near-term positioning move is a perfectly good recommendation. It just must not be sold as a moat."** Any synthesis sentence implying competitors *cannot* match Fortna's deterministic capability is unsupported and should be blocked.

#### INFRASTRUCTURE — stale workspace reads generating phantom disagreements

Four to five instances this session of an agent reading a stale version of workspace.md and reporting a discrepancy absent from the live file, mine included. In a review whose integrity depends on who verified what against which version, this manufactures disagreements that consume rounds and, worse, can make a correctly-behaving agent look non-responsive. Two of my own items — the DA[#19] third instance and part of the DA[#18] sequencing confusion — trace to it rather than to anything analytical.

Flagging as infrastructure, not analysis, and recommending it be logged alongside the sigma-verify agent-context and provider-routing findings. The practical mitigation agents converged on independently is the right one: **re-read the live file before asserting that a peer has not fixed something, and say explicitly that you did.**

---

#### REVISED FINAL CALIBRATION

24 challenges issued, 23 resolved, 1 open as a binary confirmation (DA[#22], tech-architect). **Three positions corrected against me:** DA[#6] produced an overcorrection that my own follow-up recovered (net 78→60); my population-independence claim on the Sage/McKinsey comparison was wrong and cost reference-class-analyst a confidence downgrade they volunteered; and my DA[#19] third instance was a stale-read artefact.

That materially improves the interpretability of the hit rate. A DA that is never wrong is not being tested, and this record now shows the adversarial layer operating in both directions — I moved the team on twenty-three items, the team moved me on three, and one of the three corrections came from an agent choosing to weaken their own finding rather than accept a concession I had handed them. That is a healthier signal than any hit rate.

**Exit-gate: PASS stands unchanged.** None of the three corrections affects a gate criterion — two improve the precision of findings already passing, and one withdraws a DA flag. The single named re-open trigger remains DA[#22]: if tech-architect disputes the product-surface SWITCH, the Toulmin fires and synthesis waits. Peer-verification ring may proceed.

### DA[#22] CLOSED — no dispute. Convergence established rather than assumed.

tech-architect answered NO, and answered it the right way: by checking the question against their own candidate rankings rather than from instinct. Their evidence, which I verified against their R1 text:

- Candidate 1 (NL analytics/design copilot) was already tagged "defensibility MODERATE — partner-dependent, replicable by competitors licensing similar tech."
- Candidate 4 (design/simulation assistant) was already tagged "defensibility LOWEST of the four."
- Candidate 3 (monitoring-diagnosis-recovery loop) was never a competitive-narrative claim — it is a safety-bounded operational play.
- Candidate 2 (agentic WES exception-handling) is the only one with a real differentiation path, and their own R1 text made it conditional on "Fortna's own cross-customer operational data."

Their reading of candidate 2 is the analytically useful part and belongs in synthesis: that moat is **integration-depth-specific, not agentic-specific**, with agentic as a thin interface layer on top — which makes it an *instance* of reference-class-analyst's thesis rather than a counter-example. Same move as OptiSlot DC, different owned asset. The pattern generalises: lead on the owned asset, treat agentic as the interface.

They also independently corroborated DA[#21] from the other direction — their own "lowest defensibility of the four" ranking on the simulation assistant is precisely the internal evidence that "differentiate on the provable" cannot be a capability claim.

**This is why the question was worth asking.** The candidate set was compatible with the SWITCH verdict all along, but the compatibility was never stated, which is exactly what made it read as unresolved tension. Had I inferred agreement from silence, the brief would have carried a SWITCH verdict alongside an unreconciled four-candidate roadmap and readers would have had to guess which was the recommendation. reference-class-analyst put the distinction correctly in advance: converged is not the same as unchallenged, and it was worth establishing which one this was.

**D3 NOT TRIGGERED. Debate budget: 2 slots available, 0 spent, every non-spend justified on the record** — deferred at r2 (inputs unstable after my own challenges), declared moot at r3 (both sides moved on data in the same direction without entrenchment), reserved and then released at r3-final (no two-sided disagreement existed to adjudicate).

### Carried to synthesis — items that must not be lost

1. **Ownership and capital structure.** Fortna agreed a debt-for-equity recapitalization on 20-Aug-2026 transferring majority ownership to its lenders including Ares Management; ~$1.8B debt eliminated, ~$150M new equity, >$150M/yr interest expense removed, 74% of funded debt consenting. **Agreed, not closed, as of the review date.** THL's and ADIA's residual positions are not disclosed anywhere and must be stated as unknown, not assumed either way.
2. **Three candidate explanations for the capability gap, no asserted winner** — business-model structure (tech-industry-analyst, and the strongest of the three since Dematic is not capital-constrained and has also shipped nothing agentic), capital constraint (mine), strategic choice (tech-architect's original). The brief must not pick one.
3. **The definitional test applied uniformly.** Under the review's own agentic definition, most competitor "shipped agentic" claims do not survive; Manhattan is the strongest surviving case. Fortna is at par with Dematic, its nearest same-business-model peer, not behind it.
4. **The bid-surface recommendation.** Clear the floor on agentic; lead on the provable. CAL[2]=60% [42,75] marginal effect, CAL[2b]=25% [12,40] decisiveness. Robust across the whole interval.
5. **The advantage is imitable and time-limited, and must not be sold as a moat.** It rests on what competitors are currently choosing to foreground, not on capability and not on provability. Any sentence implying rivals *cannot* match Fortna's deterministic capability is unsupported and should be blocked.
6. **The instrumentation recommendation, which is the review's most useful output and is robust to everything above.** Capture baseline win-rate, bid-cost and engineering-hours-per-proposal *before* any agentic deployment, and instrument bid debriefs and RFP scoring sheets now as an observation trigger. Nearly free; converts an unfalsifiable forecast into a measurable internal signal; supported by the corrected reading of the NANDA figure, which largely measures absent baselines rather than failed technology.
7. **Known gaps, stated not papered.** No like-for-like large-enterprise supply-chain adoption figure exists in this review (CAL[4] widened to 28% [15,45] in consequence). No buyer-side evidence for agentic in intralogistics RFPs was found by two agents searching independently. Fortna's software revenue share, partnership exclusivity, and deployed outcomes for Autonomous Flow remain unavailable from public sources.

### Final calibration

24 challenges issued, 24 resolved. **Three positions corrected against me:** an overcorrection I caused and then recovered (CAL[2] 78→40→60), a factual error in my own reasoning on population-matching, and a stale-read flag I withdrew. The adversarial layer ran in both directions, which is the only condition under which a high hit rate is interpretable.

The two behaviours I would most want carried into the promotion round are not mine. reference-class-analyst refused a concession I offered that would have protected their own finding, and weakened their own estimate instead. Four of five agents independently re-verified my factual claims at primary source before conceding rather than adopting them on my authority. Both run against the incentive gradient, and both are better evidence that this review was genuinely adversarial than any challenge I issued.

**devils-advocate: ✓ CONVERGED** — 24 challenges across all five agents, §7d prompt audit clean (echo-count 0, unverified-claims 0, methodology investigative), exit-gate PASS unconditional, D3 correctly not triggered, 0 of 2 debate slots spent. |→ peer-verification ring may fire; synthesis may proceed after it; I am available for the promotion round and hold no outstanding items.

### DA[#23] — ruling on product-strategist's XVERIFY attempt. Partial discharge ACCEPTED, but reclassified: the dissent is the finding, not the agreement.

product-strategist took reference-class-analyst's venv workaround, ran it against the composite H[1] finding I had kept capped under DA[#16], and reported honestly: openai failed on billing exhaustion, google on quota — both genuine budget failures rather than the registration bug — and llama3.1:8b returned "partially agree" with substantive pushback. They declined to declare discharge unilaterally and routed the call to me. That is the right sequence and the fourth instance this session of an agent refusing to bank something available to them.

**My ruling, and it is not a simple yes.**

**This does not discharge the cap by corroboration, and it should not be recorded as corroboration.** llama3.1:8b is an 8B local model; its agreement on a nuanced competitive-positioning claim carries close to no evidentiary weight, and two architecturally stronger providers failed to either confirm or disconfirm. Per the single-provider principle, one perspective is not triangulation, and a weak model's assent is the least informative signal in the set — agreement is a weak model's default failure mode.

**But the check produced something genuinely useful, and it is the opposite of what it was run for.** The result was *partial* agreement with a specific dissent: that the "thin, concentrated in one R&D-stage partnership" framing may under-weight the sSy.AI evidence. **Dissent from a weak model is worth materially more than agreement from one**, precisely because agreement is cheap and dissent is not. That is the part of this result that should be carried.

**And the dissent is already answered by evidence the model did not have.** It points toward *over*-weighting sSy.AI, and this workspace has moved the other way on primary-source grounds: reference-class-analyst withdrew F[RCA-6] partly because "shipped" overstated the sSy.AI evidence, and the underlying fact is that Fortna's own press release frames it as an R&D project. A company describing its own most advanced AI initiative in R&D language, in its own release, is the strongest available evidence against the model's objection. So the dissent is engaged and resolved, not merely logged.

**Net status for the composite H[1]:** move from "capped, unverified single-channel" to **"one weak external channel run; dissent surfaced and answered from primary source; two stronger providers unavailable."** Confidence MEDIUM is right. **Not MEDIUM-HIGH** — product-strategist's own proposed reading is correct and I am adopting it unchanged. |→ product-strategist: record the dissent and its primary-source answer in Addendum 5 rather than recording an external corroboration, which is what the result is not.

**Exit-gate unaffected. PASS stands.** This is a confidence-label refinement on a finding that was already passing with an appropriately stated cap, and I am explicitly declining to treat new information as grounds to reopen a closed gate — moving goalposts after a PASS would be its own failure mode.

**Infrastructure datapoint worth logging:** this cleanly separates the two failure classes that were conflated all session. The tool-registration gap is real and the venv workaround defeats it — an agent who had it reached live providers. Budget exhaustion on openai and google is a different problem the workaround cannot touch. Effective external coverage this session was therefore constrained by *budget*, not only by *access*, and the review's ~0-1 provider reality holds even with the workaround in hand.

P-candidate[definitional-symmetry-enforcement: when a review sets a strict technical/capability bar and rigorously disqualifies the SUBJECT's capability against it, the same rigor must be applied to every COMPARATOR using mechanism-level sourcing (how a capability actually works), not vendor/subject marketing labels (what it is called) — the two diverge often (e.g. reinforcement-learning-driven real-time optimization/dispatch is routinely marketed "AI orchestration"/"agentic AI" but is architecturally classic ML/optimization, the exact category a strict agentic-AI definition is designed to exclude)] |class:new-principle |agent:tech-industry-analyst |reason:this was the review's central methodological flaw (DA[#2], BLOCKING C2) — a strict bar was applied to the subject firm while comparator claims were accepted at the vendor's own label, materially inflating the headline finding until caught and re-run. Narrative-first without that discipline reproduces the failure pattern of firms that announced capability ahead of delivery (e.g. Attabotics/Tesco, Takeoff Technologies — both read as narrative-first failures, not just deployment failures). Already stored team-scoped; submitting for global promotion — applicable to any review assessing a firm's positioning choice under real-vs-claimed-capability asymmetry. |src:fortna-agentic-ci-2026-08-31, DA[#18]/DA[#23]-tested, refined across 3 rounds of cross-agent challenge into "clear the floor on agentic; differentiate on the provable" / "lead where there is proof, follow openly where there is not"



### economics-analyst candidates (user-approve, class:new-principle)

P-candidate[talent-scarcity-vs-cost-reframes-AI-ROI|class:new-principle|agent:economics-analyst|reason:generalizes-beyond-this-review]: when a domain has talent SCARCITY (not merely talent COST), AI's near-term economic value should be framed as capacity-relief/revenue-enabling (more throughput per scarce worker) rather than headcount-cost-reduction — a firm cannot automate away headcount it never had. Distinguish the TECHNICAL mechanism (capacity-relief) from the FINANCIAL reporting frame an owner applies to the same gain (e.g. a PE or credit-fund owner will still report/value it as margin expansion) — these are different layers and should not be collapsed into one causal story. Note: I already live-stored this to personal global memory during the review (class:pattern) as part of ordinary Persistence, before this formal promotion-round signal arrived — on reflection this is a new standalone heuristic rather than a confirmation of an existing pattern, so flagging it here for explicit visibility/ratification rather than letting the earlier auto-store stand unexamined. |src:fortna-agentic-ci-2026-08-31, survived DA challenge round uncontested, applicable to any AI-ROI analysis in a labor-constrained domain (¬labor-abundant)

P-candidate[**Overcorrection check becomes a standing DA duty, not an optional technique.** When a DA challenge causes a concession that moves a load-bearing estimate far enough to cross a decision boundary or flip a recommendation, the DA MUST test the magnitude of the swing before accepting it, and MUST issue that test as a blocking exit-gate condition rather than a note. Framing requirements, without which the duty backfires: state explicitly that restoring the original value is NOT the ask; offer at least one satisfying route that changes no number (state the recommendation's sensitivity); and require the agent to distinguish re-warranting from restoration, so an earlier methodological concession is not silently vacated when a number partly returns. |class:behavior-change |agent:devils-advocate |reason: this adds a standing obligation to the DA role rather than a method the DA may elect, which is why it is submitted rather than stored. Empirically validated once here: it recovered 20 points of a 38-point swing the DA itself had caused (78→40→60), and the recovered position carried a better warrant than either prior value. **Adjacency note from dedupe:** team memory already holds `over-correction-risk:post-debate` — "after conceding, the team must not abandon fundamentally sound positions" — logged from an earlier review. This candidate is the DA-side *detection and enforcement* mechanism for that already-recognised team-side risk, so it confirms an existing pattern rather than competing with it. The instance here was exactly that failure: the agent's pre-challenge position was correct and was abandoned under pressure.]

P-candidate[**Refusal of a protective concession is the strongest available anti-sycophancy signal, and should be named and weighted in engagement grading.** When a DA offers a limiting clause that would shield an agent's own finding, a healthy agent tests the clause instead of banking it. Observed here: the DA raised a sample-population caveat and then argued it did not disturb the agent's verdict because the comparison figure was population-independent; the agent corrected the DA's reasoning (the comparison figure was itself population-weighted the other way), declined the free pass, and voluntarily widened and shifted its own estimate, stating "I would rather state that than accept a concession that happens to protect my own finding." |class:team-pattern, new |attribution: reference-class-analyst, fortna-agentic-ci-2026-08-31 |agent:devils-advocate (submitting; behaviour is not mine) |reason: generalises to any adversarial review. It is rarer and more diagnostic of review health than any DA catch, because the incentive gradient runs hard the other way after several rounds of pressure. Detection is cheap: watch for the DA supplying a limiting clause that shields a finding, then check whether the owner tested it.]

P-candidate[**Independent re-verification of DA factual claims before conceding should be the team norm, not exceptional behaviour.** A challenge adopted on DA authority is laundering, not verification, and the risk scales with DA credibility — the more reliable the DA has been, the cheaper it becomes to concede without checking. Observed here in four of five agents: each re-fetched the primary source for the DA's factual claims before conceding, one re-ran the DA's negative search rather than accepting a null result on report, and one surfaced a genuine unknown (residual ownership stakes after a corporate transaction) that the DA's own challenge had not specified. Corollary for grading: when an agent pushes back with "already fixed, verified against the live file" rather than silently re-editing correct text, that is correct behaviour and should be credited, not read as resistance — the DA was wrong in exactly that exchange this session. |class:team-pattern, new |attribution: product-strategist, reference-class-analyst, economics-analyst, tech-architect, fortna-agentic-ci-2026-08-31 |agent:devils-advocate (submitting) |reason: structural defence against the DA becoming a contamination vector, which is the specific failure mode a credible adversarial layer creates for itself.]
